- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Theodora, leader of Byzantium (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Byzantium
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Cataphract, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Dromon, Replacing Liburna (Requires technology: Sailing)
        - Unique Building: Tetraconch, Replacing Temple (Requires technology: Philosophy)
        - Unique Improvement: Aplekton (Unique Byzantine replacement for the Fort.
          +50% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile, and it cannot be stolen by foreign Empires. Contains Fortifications, allowing owned Units to stay stationed in this tile after attacking and a Canal, allowing friendly Sea Units to traverse through it. Has improved yields compared to the Fort it replaces, including Food and Production.); Requires technology: Engineering
        - Civilization Ability: Can always found a Religion, receives 1 Additional Belief when founding, and may select Beliefs present in other Religions. -15% Faith purchase costs, and may purchase unlocked Great People starting in the Classical Era.
      - Leader: Theodora
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Peace, Land, Bread (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Peace, Land, Bread: -2 Unhappiness from Poverty and Growth increased by 20% in all Cities. Building maintenance reduced by 20%.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Militarism (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Culture in Cities with at least 10 Strength.
        - * -15% Unit Maintenance.
        - * -50% Route Maintenance.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 10
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 10
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 10
        - WarmongerHate: 10
        - NeutralBias: 1

        - GuardedBias: 10
        - AfraidBias: 5
        - DiplomaticBalance: 9
        - Friendliness: 10
        - WorkWithWillingness: 10
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 10
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 10
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 5
        - MinorCivWarBias: 4
        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 5
        - Meanness: 7
        - Neediness: 5
        - Chattiness: 8
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: The fall of Nicaea is a devastating blow, but our persona remains appropriate: maximum hostility and competitiveness toward the Aztecs, full commitment to allies (China, Carthage), and aggressive cultural/diplomatic pursuit. We will not surrender; we must defend our remaining cities and continue pushing cultural influence. No change needed.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Radar
        - Rationale: Radar unlocks Fighters and Heavy Bombers, essential to intercept Aztec air units and bomb their stacks. After Rocketry (4 turns), Radar is the logical next step to gain air superiority and defensive capabilities. Nuclear Fission can follow later for atomic bombs.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Foreign Service (Policy)
        - Rationale: Foreign Service provides +50% city-state quest rewards, faster Great Diplomats, and spy points. This synergizes with our Diplomacy flavor (95) and helps secure city-state alliances for extra delegates and resources, bolstering our diplomatic position and espionage against the Aztecs. People's Army can wait as immediate need is diplomatic leverage.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Venice
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Venice is defeated but we have a Declaration of Friendship. Maintaining a positive public modifier at 40 encourages any remaining diplomatic interactions and honors our past alliance. This may provide minor benefits in World Congress or if they revive.
        - UpdatedTurn: 209
        
        ### Ecbatana
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 60
        - Rationale: Ecbatana has a quest for most new followers (currently led by China). We have a trade route to them, and increasing private modifier to 60 ensures our AI continues to prioritize maintaining the route and competing for the quest, gaining influence and potential alliance.
        - UpdatedTurn: 380
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Japan is a vassal of the Aztecs and has declared war on us. They are weak but could provide flanking pressure. Setting private to -50 ensures our AI treats them as a hostile minor threat, but we should not divert significant resources from the Aztec front. Public 0 reflects no diplomatic engagement.
        - UpdatedTurn: 384
        
        ### Arabia
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Arabia is our defensive pact ally and the current cultural victory contender. They are crucial for tourism bonuses (open borders, trade routes) and World Congress votes. Maximum friendship ensures they don't become hostile or close borders, which would hurt our tourism output.
        - UpdatedTurn: 386
        
        ### Panama City
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -60
        - Rationale: Panama City declared war on us and is allied to the Aztecs. Setting public/private to -60 ensures our AI treats them as hostile and may capture them if opportunity arises, denying Aztec influence and securing a strategic location.
        - UpdatedTurn: 387
        
        ### Vancouver
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -60
        - Rationale: Vancouver declared war on us and is allied to the Aztecs. Same logic as Panama City: treat as hostile to potentially capture and deny Aztec influence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 387
        
        ### Okilis
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: We made peace with Vancouver (city-state). Setting to neutral (0) avoids unnecessary hostility while we focus on the Aztec war. We can reassess later if needed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 391
        
        ### Ife
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Peace treaty forced peace with Ife. Setting neutral modifiers avoids unnecessary hostility, allowing focus on rebuilding and cultural victory. May reconsider later.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Quebec City
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Peace treaty forced peace with Quebec City. Neutral stance to avoid conflict and concentrate on recovery and tourism.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Cape Town
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Peace treaty forced peace with Cape Town. Reset to neutral to prevent diplomatic penalties and prioritize internal development.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### The Aztecs
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Aztecs remain our existential enemy. They are attacking our ally China (Shanghai under heavy siege). Maintain maximum hostility to ensure our AI focuses all efforts on defending against them and preparing for the coming war when peace treaty ends.
        - UpdatedTurn: 400
        
        ### China
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: China is our defensive pact ally and key to holding the line. They are losing Shanghai; we must keep relations at maximum to ensure open borders, trade routes, and coordinated defense. They are also a tourism target; we need them friendly for victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 400
        
        ### Carthage
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 80
        - Rationale: Carthage is about to become Influential (1 turn). Keep strong relations for tourism, World Congress votes, and as a counterbalance to Aztecs. They are also at war with Aztecs.
        - UpdatedTurn: 400
        
        ### Denmark
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 85
        - Rationale: Denmark is neutral but has 11 delegates. Keep private high to encourage neutrality/support in World Congress. Public remains moderate due to religious differences; we don't want to provoke them.
        - UpdatedTurn: 400
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: War with the Aztecs resumes in 2 turns. We must maximize military production, air defense, and scientific output to survive and keep pace. High culture and tourism remain essential for our victory path. Production and Science increased to 100 to rush units and research Radar (Fighters). Defense, Air, and Antiair at 100 to counter Aztec air superiority and ground assaults. Gold at 100 for upgrades and purchases. Grand Strategy stays Culture to continue pushing tourism toward China and Carthage.
        - GrandStrategy: Culture

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 42
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 5 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Danish Sphere of Influence: Okilis:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Aztec Sphere of Influence: Ife:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Carthaginian Sphere of Influence: Vatican City:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Spaceflight Regulations:
            - Description: Spaceflight Regulations control the proliferation of modern ballistic technology, oversee national space programs, and limit the advance of pontentially-dangerous technology.
              As long as this resolution is active, all players will suffer a global -25% Production penalty for Spaceship Parts and +25% Gold purchase costs for Spaceship Parts.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Carthaginian Sphere of Influence: Prague:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Taoism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Sokoto:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Aztecs:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Decolonization:
            - Description: Enforce global decolonization. If successful, the Influence of all Players with all City-States is lowered to 50, unless they are permanent allies.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: China
          - Enact: Open Door: Panama City:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - Proposer: Carthage
          - Enact: Global Wargames:
            - Description: Begins the Global Wargames project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : Menin Gate wonder appears in Capital. (Receive +2 Happiness in the City in which it is built. Receive 2 Culture and Golden Age Points whenever an owned unit is killed, scaling with Era. Forts, Citadels and Landmarks provide +2 Culture and Golden Age Points when worked.)
              : Receive a +25% attack bonus to all Military Units for the next 30 turns and 4 of your military units will be maintenance-free.
              : Receive one Infantry and 3 of your military units will be maintenance-free.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Replaceable Parts.
            - Proposer: The Aztecs

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Theodora, leader of Byzantium (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Byzantium
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Cataphract, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Dromon, Replacing Liburna (Requires technology: Sailing)
        - Unique Building: Tetraconch, Replacing Temple (Requires technology: Philosophy)
        - Unique Improvement: Aplekton (Unique Byzantine replacement for the Fort.
          +50% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile, and it cannot be stolen by foreign Empires. Contains Fortifications, allowing owned Units to stay stationed in this tile after attacking and a Canal, allowing friendly Sea Units to traverse through it. Has improved yields compared to the Fort it replaces, including Food and Production.); Requires technology: Engineering
        - Civilization Ability: Can always found a Religion, receives 1 Additional Belief when founding, and may select Beliefs present in other Religions. -15% Faith purchase costs, and may purchase unlocked Great People starting in the Classical Era.
      - Leader: Theodora
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Peace, Land, Bread (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Peace, Land, Bread: -2 Unhappiness from Poverty and Growth increased by 20% in all Cities. Building maintenance reduced by 20%.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Militarism (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Culture in Cities with at least 10 Strength.
        - * -15% Unit Maintenance.
        - * -50% Route Maintenance.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Rationale: Radar is our absolute top research priority to unlock Fighters and Heavy Bombers. Fighters are critically needed to intercept Aztec Heavy Bombers and protect our land forces and cities from air devastation.
        - Technology: Radar
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Selecting Enlightenment as our next policy to receive a Free Technology. This will allow us to instantly acquire a massive late-game tech (like Nuclear Fission or Electronics) to help bridge the tech gap with the Aztecs and secure critical defensive assets.
        - Policy: Enlightenment

    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Assessing Strategic Landscape**
            
            With the Atomic Era upon us and just under 100 turns remaining, I'm now meticulously evaluating our current standing and the rapidly shrinking window of opportunity for strategic maneuvers.
            
            **Analyzing Victory Conditions**
            
            I'm currently weighing our cultural victory prospects against the overwhelming Aztec dominance. While my cultural influence is strong with many civs, the Aztecs' technological and military might presents a significant challenge. I'm focusing on accelerating cultural spread to secure this win.
            
            **Verifying Ideology Mechanics**
            
            I'm currently scrutinizing the intricacies of our ideology, specifically confirming the tier and tenets of "Order" as we approach the Citizen Earth Protocol. My immediate focus is on understanding its synergy with our victory conditions and how it impacts our relations with key players like Denmark, China, Carthage, and the Aztecs, especially given their formidable cultural output and our current tourism deficit.
            
            **Evaluating Diplomatic Threats**
            
            The Aztec capture of Shanghai is concerning. I'm now focused on their formidable military and their proximity to securing a diplomatic victory, as the World Congress convenes soon. Their delegate count and potential city-state influence pose a serious challenge.
            
            **Navigating World Congress**
            
            The "Sanction: The Aztecs" policy has passed, a welcome economic blow, though their wealth remains substantial. I'm prioritizing blocking "Open Door" to preserve our vital alliance with Panama City, which is currently allied to us. Simultaneously, I'm reviewing upcoming technology selections with Rocketry nearing completion.
            
            **Acquiring Aerial Superiority**
            
            My immediate concern is unlocking Radar. The Aztec air force, boasting Heavy Bombers, is a critical threat we must counter. Securing Fighters is paramount, and I am prioritizing this technological advancement above all else to protect our existing assets and counter their air dominance before it's too late.
            
            **Utilizing Acquired Assets**
            
            I'm now reassessing my Great Musician's presence within Aztec territory. Given the diplomatic and cultural challenges, I'm exploring the potential for a Concert Tour to directly impact the Aztec tourism deficit and accelerate my cultural victory, a tactic I hadn't fully considered until now.
            
            **Deploying Musical Assets**
            
            My Great Musician is already within Aztec borders, seemingly bypassing the need for open borders. I'm now focused on whether a Concert Tour can be initiated from this advantageous position, potentially providing the crucial tourism boost needed for a cultural victory.
            
            **Initiating Concert Tour**
            
            I'm now analyzing the tourism yield of a Concert Tour and the potential to generate Great Works through archaeologists. While my current tourism output is strong, the Aztecs' immense culture per turn necessitates strategic adjustments. I'm prioritizing spreading our shared religion and establishing trade routes, if possible, to enhance our cultural influence.
            
            **Establishing Trade Route**
            
            My immediate focus is on securing a trade route with the Aztecs. I've noted an available route and a trained Caravan, which, if peace holds or can be achieved within two turns, will grant a significant tourism modifier, potentially crucial for my victory condition.
            
            **Resolving Diplomatic Puzzles**
            
            I'm untangling a knot in our defensive pact with China and our current peace treaty with the Aztecs. It appears our forced peace overrides the pact, but this should change once our treaty with them expires in two turns, pulling us back into conflict if China remains at war.
            
            **Preparing for War**
            
            War with the Aztecs is imminent in two turns as our peace treaty expires, automatically reactivating our defensive pact with China. Their overwhelming military strength necessitates an immediate shift to all-out defense. I am re-evaluating asset deployment to mitigate their air and ground superiority before conflict erupts.
            
            **Defending Border Positions**
            
            I'm focusing on utilizing our Aplektons for their defensive bonus. With Anti-Aircraft Guns in key locations, I'm assessing their positioning to counter the Aztec air threat and secure our borders effectively.
            
            **Defending Against Bombers**
            
            My current priority is securing Radar to build Fighters and counter the Aztec air superiority. With Rockery nearly complete, my focus shifts to Interception capabilities to protect against their Heavy Bombers, necessitating a complete defense and mobilization strategy.
            
            **Adjusting Strategic Priorities**
            
            My previous focus on maximizing numerous priorities appears counterproductive. I'm now consolidating my approach, recognizing that an overly broad focus dilutes impact. I'm concentrating on a more targeted set of high-impact flavors.
            
            **Refining Strategic Flavors**
            
            I'm now paring down our strategic priorities, recognizing the need for focus. Key areas like Defense, Science, Production, Gold, Culture, and Tourism remain paramount. I'm dialing back Religion and Great People to bolster our chances against the Aztec threat.
            
            **Reallocating Strategic Resources**
            
            I'm now adjusting our strategic allocations, recognizing that diplomacy is currently unfeasible against the Aztecs and Carthage. Espionage, however, shows immense promise with multiple spies on the verge of stealing valuable technology from Aztec cities. I'm increasing our focus on espionage accordingly.
            
            **Revising Espionage Policy**
            
            I'm reassessing my choice of "Foreign Service," questioning its value. Rationalism's "Enlightenment" offering a free technology, especially with Radar or Computers within reach, seems far more impactful for our current scientific and defensive needs than the espionage bonuses.
            
            **Evaluating Policy Branches**
            
            I'm reconsidering the utility of "Foreign Service" versus the Rationalism branch, specifically "Rights of Man" and "Enlightenment." The latter promises a free technology, which seems far more beneficial for our current scientific and defensive needs against the Aztecs. I need to confirm the policy tree structure and ensure we can access Enlightenment.
            
            **Considering Ideology Options**
            
            I'm now evaluating available Order ideology tenets, specifically "Peace, Land, Bread." This could offer benefits to our science and production, potentially outweighing current policy choices like "Foreign Service," especially given the looming conflict and the need for swift technological advancement.
            
            **Selecting Ideology Tenets**
            
            I'm now reassessing the viability of "Order" ideology tenets given their accessibility. "Workers' Faculties" appears incredibly potent for production and science, potentially surpassing Rationalism's offerings and providing a crucial boost against the Aztec threat. I need to confirm their immediate impact.
            
            **Selecting Ideology Tenets**
            
            I'm now weighing the available "Order" ideology tenets, finding "Workers' Faculties" to be a standout choice for its substantial science and production bonuses. This seems to offer a superior advantage over our current "Foreign Service" policy, especially considering our looming conflict and the need for rapid technological advancement. I am confirming its immediate impact and policy tree accessibility.
            
            **Securing Free Technology**
            
            I am now confirming if "Enlightenment" from the Rationalism policy branch is accessible and can grant a free technology. This development, if achievable, would significantly accelerate our scientific progress and bolster our defenses against the looming Aztec threat, potentially overriding other policy considerations.
            
            **Choosing Next Policy**
            
            I am confirming which policy offers the greatest immediate benefit. While "Enlightenment" and "Workers' Faculties" both seem highly advantageous for science and production respectively, I'm prioritizing the one offering the most impactful boost against the Aztecs.
            
            **Choosing Next Policy**
            
            I'm confirming the accessibility and impact of "Enlightenment" from Rationalism. A free technology is paramount to catch up scientifically with the Aztecs, who are far ahead. This seems to offer the most crucial boost.
            
            **Assessing World Congress**
            
            The upcoming World Congress votes in five turns are a critical juncture. I'm evaluating proposals and our delegate count to strategize how to influence the outcomes.
            
            **Selecting Policy and Tech**
            
            I'm confirming the accessibility of "Enlightenment" for a free technology, as it seems the most crucial immediate scientific boost against the Aztecs. I'll then set research to Electronics or Nuclear Fission to further our technological edge.
            
            **Focusing on Technology**
            
            I am confirming the accessibility and immediate impact of "Enlightenment" and "Workers' Faculties" to determine the most crucial next step. A free technology from Enlightenment is paramount to bridge the scientific gap with the Aztecs, and I am setting Radar as the next research to ensure our air superiority.
            
            **Resolving Diplomatic Confusion**
            
            I'm untangling a confusing diplomatic status with Panama City. Despite a recent hostile stance due to war, they've become a powerful ally, providing valuable delegates and influence. I need to correct their relationship status from public and private hostility to reflect this new alliance.
            
            **Updating Diplomatic Standing**
            
            I am now prioritizing the recalibration of our diplomatic relationships with city-states and major powers. Specifically, I'm adjusting our standing with Panama City to reflect its renewed alliance, while also noting the shifting allegiances of other cities like Vancouver, and ensuring our relationship values accurately represent current alliances and rivalries.
            
            **Updating Diplomatic Relations**
            
            I am now meticulously adjusting our relationships with allied and neutral city-states and major powers. China remains at 100 to leverage our defensive pact against the Aztec threat, while Carthage is being maintained at 80 as a crucial ally in the ongoing conflict with the Aztecs. Panama City's relationship has been upgraded to reflect its renewed alliance.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Leverage**
            
            I'm now evaluating Denmark's denouncement of the Aztecs as a positive development, strengthening our strategic position. I'm adjusting our private relations with Denmark to 85 to reflect this alignment.
            
            **Prioritizing Defensive Capabilities**
            
            I'm now re-evaluating our strategic priorities, focusing heavily on defense and science to counter the Aztec threat. Offense, Recon, Naval, and Expansion are significantly reduced, while Espionage, Happiness, and Great People are maintained. I'm also adjusting Tile Improvement and Infrastructure values to reflect our defensive posture.
            
            **Adjusting Strategic Flavors**
            
            I'm now fine-tuning our strategic flavor priorities. Defense remains at 100, with a significant focus on Mobile and Ranged units at 50. Wonder construction is minimized to 10, and Religion is reduced to 50. Diplomacy is now at 30, and Spaceship is set to 0.
            
            **Optimizing Strategic Priorities**
            
            I'm now solidifying our strategic direction by applying the refined flavor priorities. Defense remains paramount, with a strong emphasis on mobile and ranged units. Wonder construction and religion are de-emphasized, while diplomacy is being carefully managed. I am also confirming the persona is aligned with our current aggressive stance against the Aztecs.
            
            **Correcting Diplomatic Application**
            
            I've realized my application of the `set-relationship` function was flawed. It's strictly for major civilizations, not city-states like Panama City. My previous attempts to adjust its standing were thus ineffective.
            
            **Clarifying Diplomatic Roles**
            
            I'm now verifying the exact designations of players, realizing city-states are indeed minor civilizations. This clarifies my previous error with Panama City; `set-relationship` is only for major civilizations, and I have no further adjustments needed for Denmark, Aztecs, Carthage, or China.
            
            **Verifying Major Civs**
            
            I'm confirming the active major civilizations and my understanding of their current status, particularly Japan's vassalage. This verification clarifies that direct relationship adjustments using the `set-relationship` function are not needed for any major powers this turn.
            
            **Applying Strategic Flavors**
            
            I am now applying the refined strategic flavors, prioritizing survival and defense against the Aztecs while maintaining our cultural victory trajectory. This involves setting `GrandStrategy` to `Culture` to keep our primary victory condition in focus.
            
            **Prioritizing Air Defense**
            
            I'm now focused on maximizing our air capabilities and defense. With Radar research nearing completion, I'm setting air priorities to 100 to ensure we can counter the Aztec heavy bombers. Nuke development is also at 100 for deterrence.
            
            **Prioritizing Air Defense**
            
            My current focus is on bolstering our aerial capabilities and defense. With Radar research nearly complete, I'm setting air priorities to 100 to effectively counter the Aztec heavy bombers. Additionally, nuclear development is at 100 for maximum deterrence.
            
            **Choosing Next Policy**
            
            I'm confirming the accessibility and impact of "Enlightenment" from Rationalism. A free technology is paramount to bridge the scientific gap with the Aztecs, who are far ahead. This seems to offer the most crucial boost.
            
            **Selecting Next Policy**
            
            I am confirming the accessibility and impact of "Enlightenment" from Rationalism. A free technology is paramount to bridge the scientific gap with the Aztecs, who are far ahead. This seems to offer the most crucial boost.
            
            **Securing Key Policies**
            
            I'm confirming "Enlightenment" is accessible and will grant a free technology, crucial for bridging the scientific gap with the Aztecs. With Radar research set as my next goal, this free tech will significantly bolster our defenses.
            
            **Selecting Key Policies**
            
            I'm confirming "Enlightenment" is accessible and will grant a free technology, crucial for bridging the scientific gap with the Aztecs. With Radar research set as my next goal, this free tech will significantly bolster our defenses.
            
            **Securing Critical Policies**
            
            I'm confirming "Enlightenment" is accessible and will grant a free technology, crucial for bridging the scientific gap with the Aztecs. With Radar research set as my next goal, this free tech will significantly bolster our defenses and prepare for impending conflict.
